<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the spherical magnetization law for the three-dimensional nearest-neighbor isotropic quantum Heisenberg ferromagnet at every fixed positive quantum spin and every sufficiently low fixed positive temperature. As the even periodic cubes grow, the symmetric zero-field magnetization converges in moments to a uniform direction with a deterministic positive magnitude. This magnitude equals the right derivative at zero field of the infinite-volume pressure. The law is expressed through self-adjoint linear combinations of the spin components and requires no joint measurement of noncommuting observables.

- **claim-spherical-magnetization-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For each fixed $S$ there is a finite $\beta_0(S)$ such that, for every fixed finite $\beta\ge\beta_0(S)$, one has $m=m_{S,\beta}>0$ and $$\label{eq:spherical-law} \lim_{\substack{L\to\infty\\L\text{ even}}} \left\langle \exp\!\left(\frac1V\sum_{a=1}^3t_aM_L^a\right) \right\rangle_{\beta,L} =\frac1{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb S^2}e^{m t\cdot n}\,\mathrm d\sigma(n) =\frac{\sinh(m\|t\|)}{m\|t\|} \qquad(t\in\mathbb R^3).$$ Here $\sigma$ is surface-area measure on $\mathbb S^2$, and the quotient at $t=0$ is interpreted as $1$. In addition, $$\label{eq:second-moment-law} \lim_{\substack{L\to\infty\\L\text{ even}}} \frac1{V^2}\sum_{x,y\in\Lambda_L} \langle S_x\cdot S_y\rangle_{\beta,L}=m^2.$$
  - Explanation: At low enough fixed temperature the symmetric finite-volume zero-field state has a magnetization with deterministic positive length m and uniform direction. The moment transform converges to the spherical average, and the normalized sum of pair correlations converges to the square of m.
- **claim-finite-pin-inputs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pin-inputs)): For the finite picture above the following assertions hold.* 1. *For every finite $E$ and nonempty single-time $T$ disjoint from $E$, $$\label{eq:simultaneous-pin} \mathbb P_E(T\subseteq\mathcal B_E) \le(2r(E,T))^{\|T\|}.$$* 2. *Almost surely under $\mathbb P_P$, $g_P(\mathbf a):=\mathbb Q_{\mathbf a}(G_P)>0$, and the conditional law of the remaining picture is $\mathbb Q_{\mathbf a}(\,\cdot\mid G_P)$.* 3. *Let $\omega_{\rm m}$ be the Poisson mark counting measure and let $\lambda_{\rm b}$ be edge rate times Lebesgue time measure. For every integer $j\ge1$ and nonnegative measurable function $F$ of $j$ mark locations, $$\label{eq:factorial-domination} \mathbb E_E\sum_{(u_1,\ldots,u_j)\in\omega_{\rm m,\ne}^j} F(u_1,\ldots,u_j) \le 2^j\int F\,d\lambda_{\rm b}^{\otimes j}.$$ The sum is over ordered distinct marks, and may be restricted to any prescribed time order. Independent additional fresh Poisson clocks may be included with their own, undoubled, factorial intensity.
  - Explanation: Three finite facts for the pinned exchange picture: a simultaneous-avoidance bound in terms of the return probability, positivity of the all-hit weight together with the conditional law after exposure, and factorial domination of Poisson mark sums.
- **claim-conditional-next-pin-kernel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-next-pin)): Let $\Pi$ have an admissible permutation law on a finite set $\mathcal V=I\sqcup D$, and put $K=\mathbb E\Pi$. Let $G_I$ be the event that every permutation cycle meets $I$. On $G_I$, let $\Sigma$ be the permutation matrix on $I$ taking each pin to its next positive visit to $I$. Then $$\label{eq:all-hit} \Pr(G_I)=\det(I_D-K_{DD}).$$ For every complex matrix $W$ on $I$, there is the polynomial identity $$\label{eq:next-pin-determinant} \mathbb E[\mathbf 1_{G_I}\det(I_I-W\Sigma)] =\det\begin{pmatrix} I_I-WK_{II}&-WK_{ID}\\ -K_{DI}&I_D-K_{DD} \end{pmatrix}.$$ If $\Pr(G_I)>0$ and $I\ne\varnothing$, then $K_{DD}$ is transient and $$\label{eq:next-pin-mean} \mathbb E[\Sigma\mid G_I] =K_{II}+K_{ID}(I_D-K_{DD})^{-1}K_{DI}.$$ This is the first positive hitting kernel of $I$ for the Markov chain with independent transitions $K$. Empty determinants are one and empty correction terms are zero. In particular <a href="#eq:next-pin-determinant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:next-pin-determinant">[eq:next-pin-determinant]</a>, and its linear coefficients in $W$, remain valid when the all-hit probability is zero.
  - Explanation: A determinant identity gives the all-hit probability and the mean next-pin matrix of an admissible permutation. The conditional first moment is the first positive hitting kernel of a Markov chain with independent transitions.
- **claim-zero-free-layered-traces** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-trace-zero-free)): On finitely many qubit slots, take a finite cyclic product of permutation layers and tensor products of single-slot matrices, with at least one permutation layer. Each permutation layer is a nonzero scalar times the mean permutation operator of an admissible law. Between every two successive permutation layers, suppose that the single-slot matrix on every slot induces a projective map $$(1,x)^T\longmapsto a(x)(1,\phi(x))^T$$ with $a(x)\ne0$ and $\|\phi(x)\|<1$ for all $\|x\|\le1$. Then the trace of the cyclic product is nonzero.
  - Explanation: A cyclic product of permutation layers and single-slot matrices that strictly contract the disk has nonzero trace.
- **claim-single-slot-maps** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-strip-maps)): In this basis, matrices in $SL_2(\mathbb R)$, real in the $s^3$ coordinates, act by automorphisms of the unit disk. If $\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}z>0$, the matrix $e^{zs^2}$ sends the closed disk strictly into the open disk. There are absolute constants $b_*,a_0>0$ such that, whenever $0<b\le b_*$, $f\in[-1,1]$, and $\|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z\|<a_0$, the matrix $$\label{eq:strip-slot-map} e^{bs^2/2}\,e^{bzfs^3}\,e^{bs^2/2}$$ also sends the closed disk strictly into the open disk, with its projective coordinate defined throughout the closed disk.
  - Explanation: Real determinant-one matrices act as disk automorphisms, the imaginary-axis exponential contracts the disk strictly, and a sandwiched slot map with small complex field in a strip still contracts the disk strictly.
- **claim-sparse-pin-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-sparse-pins)): Fix $S$ and $C_s$. There is $\beta_0<\infty$ such that, for every fixed $\beta\ge\beta_0$, there is $L_*(\beta)<\infty$ with the following property. For every even $L\ge L_*(\beta)$, every $q$ satisfying $L^{-5/2}\le q<1$, every pin set $E$ as above, and every sparse set $T$ of real slot points at one deterministic time, $$\label{eq:sparse-pins} \mathbb P_E(T\subseteq\mathcal B_E)\le p^{\|T\|}, \qquad p=\beta^{-9/10}.$$ The constants and thresholds are independent of the number, locations, and times of the extra pins.
  - Explanation: On the torus, a sparse set of real slot points at one time lies on cycles avoiding all pins with probability decaying geometrically in the size of the set, uniformly in the extra pins.
- **claim-lattice-nash-unit-flows** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-lattice-inputs)): In an available-slot graph regular at $z$ with radius $A\ge A_0$, put $$\mathcal D(v)=\tfrac12\sum_{\{i,j\}\text{ available edge}} (v_i-v_j)^2.$$ Every nonnegative $v$ supported over $B_A(z)$ satisfies $$\label{eq:local-nash} \lVert v\rVert_2^{10/3}\le C\mathcal D(v)\lVert v\rVert_1^{4/3}.$$ For every available slot $a$ at $y\in B_A(z)$, there exists an oriented edge flow $F_a$, with divergence defined as outgoing minus incoming flow, such that $$\label{eq:unit-flow} \mathop{\mathrm{div}}F_a=\mathbf 1_{\{a\}},\qquad \|F_a(e)\|\le C(1+\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(e,y))^{-k},\qquad k=2-3\nu=199/100.$$ Here $\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(e,y)$ is the minimum supremum distance to the two endpoint sites. The constants are uniform over the graph, $A$, $z$, and the source. Sites with any number of available slots between one and $\ell$ are permitted.
  - Explanation: Imported deterministic lattice facts: a Nash inequality for functions supported near a regular anchor and unit flows from each available slot with power-law decay.
- **claim-blocking-component-tails** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-torus-blocking)): Under $\mathsf I(P)$, at any deterministic time $t$, $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb P_P(A'\subseteq\mathcal O_t)&\le Cp^6 &&\text{for six prescribed distinct sites},\label{eq:six-blocking}\\ \mathbb P_P(A'\subseteq\mathcal O_t)&\le(C\sqrt p)^{\|A'\|} &&\text{for a nonempty sparse set of sites}.\label{eq:sparse-blocking} \end{aligned}$$ There are $c,C,\lambda>0$, uniform for sufficiently large $\beta$, such that the blocked component formed with steps of supremum length at most eight satisfies $$\label{eq:torus-component-tail} \mathbb P_P\{z\in\mathcal O_t,\ \max_{v\in\mathcal C_t(z)}d_L(z,v)\ge h\} \le C e^{-ch^\lambda}\qquad(h\ge1).$$ All constants are independent of the grid and its size.
  - Explanation: Under the inductive hypothesis, simultaneously blocking prescribed sites is unlikely, and blocked components have stretched-exponential tails in diameter.
- **claim-regularity-localized-diffusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-torus-diffusion)): Assume $\mathsf I(P)$ and <a href="#eq:sparse-localization-radius" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sparse-localization-radius">[eq:sparse-localization-radius]</a>. For every deterministic time and anchor, failure of regularity has probability at most $$\label{eq:torus-irregularity} \Delta_A=CA^3p^6+C\exp(-cA^{\lambda\nu}).$$ Call an exposure good at the anchor if its nonregular times have total length at most $\beta/100$. Its probability of not being good is at most $100\Delta_A$. On a good exposure, the one-turn kernel localized by killing outside $B_A(z)$ has every entry at most $C\beta^{-3/2}$, in either direction.* *If a deterministic starting slot is a hole almost surely, localization at its site loses at most $Ce^{-c\beta}$ expected mass in any forward or reversed traversal piece of length at most one turn. This last assertion does not require $\mathsf I(P)$.
  - Explanation: Failure of regularity is rare, good exposures have uniformly small localized one-turn kernel entries, and localization loses little mass.
- **claim-sparse-source-pairing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-torus-flow-pairing)): At a fixed slice let $(z_i)_{i=1}^{m_T}$ be a sparse list, including repetitions. Retain any subset of its members at which the lifted graph is regular with radius $A$. For each retained member let $\mu_i\ge0$ be a mass at most one, supported on the real holes over $B_A(z_i)$. If $L$ satisfies <a href="#eq:sparse-localization-radius" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sparse-localization-radius">[eq:sparse-localization-radius]</a> and is sufficiently large compared with $A$, then every real vector $Y'$ on the holes of that slice satisfies $$\label{eq:torus-flow-pairing} \left\|\left\langle\sum_i\mu_i,Y'\right\rangle\right\| \le (Cm_TA^\alpha)^{1/2}\sqrt{\mathcal D_t(Y')} +Cm_TL^{1/2-k}\lVert Y'\rVert_2.$$
  - Explanation: Sparse localized sources paired with a hole vector are bounded by a Dirichlet-energy term plus a small divergence error from cutting off the flows.
- **claim-positive-magnetization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-positive-magnetization)): For every fixed $S$ and all sufficiently large finite $\beta$, $$m_{S,\beta}\ge S(1-\beta^{-9/10})>0.$$
  - Explanation: The sparse-pin bound gives an explicit lower bound on the magnetization at large inverse temperature, hence positivity.
- **claim-weak-field-estimate** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-weak-field)): Fix $S$. For all sufficiently large $\beta$ there are $C_\beta,R_0(\beta)<\infty$ with the following property. Let $L$ be even, let $R_0(\beta)\le R\le L$, and partition $\Lambda_L$ into rectangular cells whose side lengths belong to $[R/2,4R]$. For every deterministic real $f:\Lambda_L\to[-1,1]$ with $\sum_{x\in B}f_x=0$ on each cell $B$, put $$D_f=\sum_x f_x Z_x,\qquad q=R^{-5/2},\qquad w=1-q=e^{-b}.$$ If $\langle\cdot\rangle_b$ is the Gibbs expectation with exponent $H_0+b\sum_xY_x$, then $$\label{eq:weak-field} \frac qV\langle D_f^2\rangle_b\le C_\beta R^{-1/40}.$$ The constants are uniform in the partition, $f$, and $L$.
  - Explanation: For zero-sum cell tests the normalized transverse square in a small field is bounded by a negative power of the cell scale.
- **claim-soft-pin-identities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-soft-resolvent)): Given the exposure $\mathbf a$, for $i,j\in\mathcal H$, $i\ne j$, $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb P(i\text{ is pinned}\mid\mathbf a) &=q(D^{-1})_{ii},\label{eq:pin-marginal}\\ \mathbb P(i,j\text{ are pinned, next pin from }i\text{ is }j \mid\mathbf a) &=\frac{q^2}{w}(D^{-1})_{ij}.\label{eq:pin-successor} \end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $$\label{eq:resolvent-trace} \mathbb E\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}D^{-1}\le N.$$ For $f_i=f_{x_i}$ on the slots, $$\label{eq:transverse-resolvent} \langle D_f^2\rangle_b =\frac14\sum_i f_i^2+ \frac12\mathbb E\sum_{\substack{i,j\in\mathcal H\\i\ne j}} f_if_j(D^{-1})_{ij}.$$ All expectations here include the normalized picture and soft-pin law.
  - Explanation: Pin marginals, next-pin probabilities, the trace bound and the transverse square are expressed through the hole resolvent.
- **claim-hole-cancellation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hole-cancellation)): For every cell $B$, $$\label{eq:hole-cancellation} \mathbb E\left\|\sum_{\substack{i\in\mathcal H\\x_i\in B}}f_i\right\| \le C\sqrt{\|B\|/q}.$$
  - Explanation: After exposure the zero-sum coefficients restricted to holes in a cell still cancel up to a square-root error.
- **claim-communication-costs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-skeleton-graph)): An endpoint edge from $i$ to $j$ satisfies $(u_i-u_j)^2\le C_\beta e_i$. For a contact edge witnessed by a fixed-position interval of length $\tau\le1$, $$\label{eq:contact-cost} (u_i-u_j)^2\le C_\beta\tau^{-1}(e_i+e_j).$$ The kernel $K$ is closed on every connected component of $\Gamma$, and $$\label{eq:component-count} \mathbb E\,\#\{\text{components of }\Gamma\}\le qN.$$
  - Explanation: Differences of the resolvent vector across endpoint and contact edges are controlled by local energy, the kernel is closed on each component, and the expected number of components is small.
- **claim-bad-box-tails** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-bad-box-tail)): There are $\beta_0,c,C,\lambda_1>0$ and, for each $\beta\ge\beta_0$, a number $\delta_0(\beta)>0$ such that for every $0<\delta\le\delta_0(\beta)$, uniformly in the admissible volumes and pin parameters of this section, $$\label{eq:bad-box-tail} \mathbb P\{z\text{ is bad and its bad component reaches distance }h \text{ from }z\} \le C e^{-ch^{\lambda_1}}.$$ A specified box has a short-time flag with probability at most $C_\beta\delta$.
  - Explanation: Bad coarse boxes form components with stretched-exponential tails, and a short-time flag in a given box is rare.
- **claim-local-paths-good-boxes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-good-box-paths)): Choose a representative cut hole $i(z)$ in every good box $z$. Any two cut holes in one good box, and representative holes in two nearest-neighbor good boxes, can be joined in $\Gamma$ using at most $C_\beta$ contact edges, all with contact duration at least $\delta$, whose cut starts stay within fine distance $C_\beta$ of the boxes.* *Moreover, if a spatial realization of any edge of $\Gamma$ meets a good box $z$, its whole realization lies within fine distance $d/2$ of a point in that box, after increasing the fixed lower threshold on $d$. In particular both cut endpoint boxes have coarse distance at most one from $z$.
  - Explanation: Cut holes in good boxes are joined by few contact edges with long enough contact, and any realization meeting a good box stays local.
- **claim-hull-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hull-comparison)): There is a constant $C_\beta$ such that the following hold for every usable $B$. For fine nearest neighbors $x,x'$ within its lift, $$\label{eq:comparison-edge} \|U_B(x)-U_B(x')\|^2 \le C_\beta n^6\delta^{-1} \sum_{\substack{i\in\mathcal H\\ \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x_i,x)\le C_\beta n}} e_i.$$ For a cut hole $j$ above $x\in B$, the same right side bounds $\|u_j-U_B(x)\|^2$, unless either* 1. *there is a short-time flag in some box at coarse distance at most $100n$ from $z_x$; or* 2. *the $\Gamma$-component of $j$ has at most $C_\beta n^3$ vertices.* *The distances in the energy sum may be taken on the torus.
  - Explanation: The comparison function varies across a cell by local energy, and holes agree with it outside two exceptional situations.
- **claim-cellwise-resolvent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cell-resolvent)): There are random numbers $c_B\in[-1,1]$, one per cell, such that $$\label{eq:cell-resolvent} \mathbb E\sum_B\sum_{\substack{i\in\mathcal H\\x_i\in B}} (u_i-c_B)^2\le C_\beta N R^{-1/20}.$$
  - Explanation: The resolvent vector is approximated by one random constant per cell with a small mean square error.
- **claim-strip-curvature** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-strip-curvature)): Let $F$ be holomorphic on $\{z\in\mathbb C:\|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z\|<a_0\}$, real and even on the real axis, with $F(0)=0$. Suppose that, for some $K<\infty$, $$0\le F(x)\le K\|x\|, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Re}}F(x+iy)\le F(x) \quad(x\in\mathbb R,\ \|y\|<a_0).$$ For each $a\in(0,a_0)$ there is a strictly positive Schwartz function $J_a$ such that, with $g_a(x)=F(x)-\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}F(x+ia)$, $$\label{eq:strip-convolution} F''=J_a*g_a, \qquad J_a=\frac{2}{a^2}(h_a*h_a), \qquad h_a(x)=\frac{\pi}{2a}\mathop{\mathrm{sech}}^2\!\left(\frac{\pi x}{a}\right).$$ In particular, with $c_a=2\pi/a$, $$\label{eq:strip-curvature-growth} 0\le F''(x)\le e^{c_a\|x\|}F''(0), \qquad F(x)\le \frac{F''(0)}{c_a^2} \bigl(e^{c_a\|x\|}-1-c_a\|x\|\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: For a strip-holomorphic function with the stated sign condition the second derivative is a convolution with a positive kernel, which gives exponential growth control of the curvature on the real line.
- **claim-zero-field-exponential-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-zero-field)): Fix $S$ and a sufficiently large finite $\beta$. There are $R_0=R_0(S,\beta)$, $C_\beta<\infty$, and $\kappa>0$ with the following property. Let $L\ge R\ge R_0$, with $L$ even, and partition $\Lambda_L$ into rectangular cells whose side lengths lie in $[R/2,4R]$. If $f:\Lambda_L\to[-1,1]$ has sum zero on every cell, then, for $q=R^{-5/2}$, $b=-\log(1-q)$, and any spin component $a$, $$\label{eq:zero-exp} \log\left\langle \exp\left(bx\sum_{z\in\Lambda_L}f_zS_z^a\right) \right\rangle \le C_\beta bV, \qquad x\in\mathbb R,\quad \|x\|\le\kappa\log R.$$ Consequently there are $A_\beta,c>0$ such that the spectral measurement of $D_f=\sum_z f_zS_z^a$ in the zero-field state satisfies $$\label{eq:zero-tail} \mathbb P\left\{\|D_f\|>\frac{A_\beta V}{\log R}\right\} \le 2e^{-cbV}.$$ All bounds are uniform over the indicated partitions and tests.
  - Explanation: At zero field, zero-sum cell tests have an exponential moment bound on a logarithmically long range, giving a tail bound for their spectral measurement.
- **claim-scale-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-scale-comparison)): Fix $S$ and a sufficiently large finite $\beta$. For every sufficiently large fixed $R_*$ and all sufficiently large even $L$, there is a deterministic rectangular partition $\mathcal P_*(L)$ of $\Lambda_L$ whose side lengths lie in $[R_*/2,4R_*^{10/9}]$ and such that, for $a=1,2,3$, $$\label{eq:spatial-comparison} \frac1V\sum_{C\in\mathcal P_*(L)}\|C\| \left\langle(\overline S_C^a)^2\right\rangle \le \left\langle(M_L^a/V)^2\right\rangle +\frac{C_\beta}{\log R_*}+o_L(1).$$ Here $o_L(1)\to0$ at fixed $R_*$ and $\beta$.
  - Explanation: Block averages on a deterministic fine partition have a weighted mean square no larger than the global one up to an error that vanishes as the terminal scale grows.
- **claim-variational-framework** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-variational-framework)): The mean entropy exists on $\mathcal S_{\rm TI}$, is upper semicontinuous and affine, and $$\label{eq:variational-pressure} P_a(t)=\max_{\omega\in\mathcal S_{\rm TI}} \{\mathfrak s(\omega)+e_0(\omega)+t\omega(a)\}.$$ In particular, writing $P_0=\beta p_{S,\beta}(0)$, the set $$\mathcal E_\beta= \{\omega\in\mathcal S_{\rm TI}: \mathfrak s(\omega)+e_0(\omega)=P_0\}$$ is a nonempty compact face of $\mathcal S_{\rm TI}$. Every local limit of periodic zero-field Gibbs states belongs to $\mathcal E_\beta$, and $$\label{eq:pressure-tangent} P_a'(0+)=\max_{\omega\in\mathcal E_\beta}\omega(a).$$ The extreme points of $\mathcal E_\beta$ are precisely its states that are extreme in $\mathcal S_{\rm TI}$; we call these states ergodic.
  - Explanation: Mean entropy is affine and upper semicontinuous, pressure is a maximum over invariant states, the equilibrium set is a compact face, and pressure tangents are maxima over it.
- **claim-ergodic-averages** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-ergodic-variance)): If $\omega$ is an extreme translation-invariant state and $A$ is a self-adjoint local observable, then $$\lim_{n\to\infty} \omega\left[\left( \frac1{\|Q_n\|}\sum_{x\in Q_n}\tau_x(A)-\omega(A)\mathbf 1 \right)^2\right]=0.$$
  - Explanation: For an extreme invariant state, volume averages of a local observable concentrate on its expectation in mean square.
- **claim-replica-cross-terms** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-replica-cross)): Fix $S$ and a sufficiently large finite $\beta$. Let $\omega$ be any translation-invariant equilibrium state of $j$ unperturbed replicas. For distinct replicas $r,s$, axes $a,b$, and finitely supported real slot coefficients $v,w$ with $\sum_i v_i=\sum_i w_i=0$, $$\omega\bigl((B_v^a)^{[r]}(B_w^b)^{[s]}\bigr)=0.$$ The same holds for every finite linear combination of translates of such products.
  - Explanation: Products of compressed zero-sum observables in distinct replicas have zero expectation in every replicated equilibrium state.
- **claim-harmonic-pressure-tangent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-harmonic-tangent)): For every integer $B\ge2$ there is $D_B>0$ such that, for every integer $W\ge2B$, the function in <a href="#eq:square-tangent" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:square-tangent">[eq:square-tangent]</a> has a harmonic extension $v_{B,W}$ from the positive real axis to $\mathbb H=\{z\in\mathbb C:\Re z>0\}$ satisfying $$v_{B,W}(x)=s_{B,W}(x)\quad(x>0),\qquad v_{B,W}(z)\le s_{B,W}(\Re z)\quad(z\in\mathbb H). \label{eq:harmonic-tangent}$$
  - Explanation: The pressure tangent of the negative-square observable extends from the positive axis to a harmonic function on the right half-plane bounded by its real-part evaluation.
- **claim-harmonic-selection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-harmonic-selection)): Let $s:(0,\infty)\to\mathbb R$ be locally Lipschitz, and enumerate a countable dense subset $D=\{x_1,x_2,\ldots\}$ of $(0,\infty)$ with $x_1=1$. Suppose $F_n$ are random holomorphic functions that are almost surely nonzero on $\mathbb H$, and $T_n\to\infty$. Assume, locally uniformly for $z\in\mathbb H$, that $$\limsup_{n\to\infty} \left\{T_n^{-1}\log\mathbb E\|F_n(z)\|^2-2s(\Re z)\right\}\le0. \label{eq:selection-upper}$$ For every $q\ge1$, suppose also that, with $U_n=\prod_{i=1}^q\|F_n(x_i)\|^2$, $$T_n^{-1}\log\mathbb EU_n\longrightarrow2\sum_i s(x_i),\qquad T_n^{-1}\log\mathbb EU_n^2\longrightarrow4\sum_i s(x_i). \label{eq:selection-products}$$ Then there is a harmonic function $v$ on $\mathbb H$ with $v(x)=s(x)$ for every $x>0$ and $v(z)\le s(\Re z)$ everywhere.
  - Explanation: Moment bounds on random zero-free holomorphic functions select a harmonic limit that agrees with the given function on the positive axis and is bounded by it elsewhere.
- **claim-equilibrium-magnetization-radius** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-equilibrium-radius)): Every ergodic translation-invariant state $\omega\in\mathcal E_\beta$ satisfies $$\omega(X_0)^2+\omega(Y_0)^2+\omega(Z_0)^2=m^2. \label{eq:equilibrium-radius}$$
  - Explanation: Every ergodic equilibrium state has magnetization vector of length exactly the pressure-defined magnetization.
- **claim-local-second-moment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-second-moment)): For every finite nonempty rectangle $C\subset\mathbb Z^3$ and every translation-invariant equilibrium state $\omega$, $$\label{eq:local-second-moment} \sum_{a=1}^3\omega\!\left[ \left(\frac1{\|C\|}\sum_{x\in C}S_x^a\right)^2\right]\ge m^2.$$
  - Explanation: The summed second moment of a block spin average is bounded below by the square of the magnetization in every invariant equilibrium state.
- **claim-global-second-moment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-global-second-moment)): The periodic zero-field Gibbs states satisfy $$\label{eq:global-second-lower} \liminf_{L\to\infty}\frac1{V^2} \sum_{a=1}^3\langle(M_L^a)^2\rangle\ge m^2.$$
  - Explanation: The normalized global second moment of the total spin in periodic zero-field Gibbs states is asymptotically at least the square of the magnetization.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-next-pin",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Pi$ have an admissible permutation law on a finite set $\\mathcal V=I\\sqcup D$, and put $K=\\mathbb E\\Pi$. Let $G_I$ be the event that every permutation cycle meets $I$. On $G_I$, let $\\Sigma$ be the permutation matrix on $I$ taking each pin to its next positive visit to $I$. Then $$\\label{eq:all-hit}\n                    \\Pr(G_I)=\\det(I_D-K_{DD}).$$ For every complex matrix $W$ on $I$, there is the polynomial identity $$\\label{eq:next-pin-determinant}\n \\mathbb E[\\mathbf 1_{G_I}\\det(I_I-W\\Sigma)]\n  =\\det\\begin{pmatrix}\n       I_I-WK_{II}&-WK_{ID}\\\\\n       -K_{DI}&I_D-K_{DD}\n       \\end{pmatrix}.$$ If $\\Pr(G_I)>0$ and $I\\ne\\varnothing$, then $K_{DD}$ is transient and $$\\label{eq:next-pin-mean}\n \\mathbb E[\\Sigma\\mid G_I]\n    =K_{II}+K_{ID}(I_D-K_{DD})^{-1}K_{DI}.$$ This is the first positive hitting kernel of $I$ for the Markov chain with independent transitions $K$. Empty determinants are one and empty correction terms are zero. In particular <a href=\"#eq:next-pin-determinant\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:next-pin-determinant\">[eq:next-pin-determinant]</a>, and its linear coefficients in $W$, remain valid when the all-hit probability is zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-next-pin",

Empty determinants are one and empty correction terms are zero. In particular <a href=\"#eq:next-pin-determinant\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:next-pin-determinant\">[eq:next-pin-determinant]</a>, and its linear coefficients in $W$, remain valid when the all-hit probability is zero.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-trace-zero-free",
        "exact_quote": "On finitely many qubit slots, take a finite cyclic product of permutation layers and tensor products of single-slot matrices, with at least one permutation layer. Each permutation layer is a nonzero scalar times the mean permutation operator of an admissible law. Between every two successive permutation layers, suppose that the single-slot matrix on every slot induces a projective map $$(1,x)^T\\longmapsto a(x)(1,\\phi(x))^T$$ with $a(x)\\ne0$ and $|\\phi(x)|<1$ for all $|x|\\le1$. Then the trace of the cyclic product is nonzero.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.3"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-strip-maps",
        "exact_quote": "In this basis, matrices in $SL_2(\\mathbb R)$, real in the $s^3$ coordinates, act by automorphisms of the unit disk. If $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Re}}z>0$, the matrix $e^{zs^2}$ sends the closed disk strictly into the open disk. There are absolute constants $b_*,a_0>0$ such that, whenever $0<b\\le b_*$, $f\\in[-1,1]$, and $|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Im}}z|<a_0$, the matrix $$\\label{eq:strip-slot-map}\n           e^{bs^2/2}\\,e^{bzfs^3}\\,e^{bs^2/2}$$ also sends the closed disk strictly into the open disk, with its projective coordinate defined throughout the closed disk.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.3"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-sparse-pins",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $S$ and $C_s$. There is $\\beta_0<\\infty$ such that, for every fixed $\\beta\\ge\\beta_0$, there is $L_*(\\beta)<\\infty$ with the following property. For every even $L\\ge L_*(\\beta)$, every $q$ satisfying $L^{-5/2}\\le q<1$, every pin set $E$ as above, and every sparse set $T$ of real slot points at one deterministic time, $$\\label{eq:sparse-pins}\n \\mathbb P_E(T\\subseteq\\mathcal B_E)\\le p^{|T|},\n \\qquad p=\\beta^{-9/10}.$$ The constants and thresholds are independent of the number, locations, and times of the extra pins.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-sparse-pins",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $S$ and $C_s$. There is $\\beta_0<\\infty$ such that, for every fixed $\\beta\\ge\\beta_0$, there is $L_*(\\beta)<\\infty$ with the following property.           "source_id": "PM-271.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $S$ and $C_s$. There is $\\beta_0<\\infty$ such that, for every fixed $\\beta\\ge\\beta_0$, there is $L_*(\\beta)<\\infty$ with the following property.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-lattice-inputs",
        "exact_quote": "In an available-slot graph regular at $z$ with radius $A\\ge A_0$, put $$\\mathcal D(v)=\\tfrac12\\sum_{\\{i,j\\}\\text{ available edge}}\n                              (v_i-v_j)^2.$$ Every nonnegative $v$ supported over $B_A(z)$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:local-nash}\n \\lVert v\\rVert_2^{10/3}\\le C\\mathcal D(v)\\lVert v\\rVert_1^{4/3}.$$ For every available slot $a$ at $y\\in B_A(z)$, there exists an oriented edge flow $F_a$, with divergence defined as outgoing minus incoming flow, such that $$\\label{eq:unit-flow}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}F_a=\\mathbf 1_{\\{a\\}},\\qquad\n |F_a(e)|\\le C(1+\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(e,y))^{-k},\\qquad\n k=2-3\\nu=199/100.$$ Here $\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(e,y)$ is the minimum supremum distance to the two endpoint sites. The constants are uniform over the graph, $A$, $z$, and the source. Sites with any number of available slots between one and $\\ell$ are permitted.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-lattice-inputs",
          "exact_quote": "In an available-slot graph regular at $z$ with radius $A\\ge A_0$, put $$\\mathcal D(v)=\\tfrac12\\sum_{\\{i,j\\}\\text{ available edge}}\n                              (v_i-v_j)^2.$$ Every nonnegative $v$ supported over $B_A(z)$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:local-nash}\n \\lVert v\\rVert_2^{10/3}\\le C\\mathcal D(v)\\lVert v\\rVert_1^{4/3}.$$ For every available slot $a$ at $y\\in B_A(z)$, there exists an oriented edge flow $F_a$, with divergence defined as outgoing minus incoming flow, such that $$\\label{eq:unit-flow}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}F_a=\\mathbf 1_{\\{a\\}},\\qquad\n |F_a(e)|\\le C(1+\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(e,y))^{-k},\\qquad\n k=2-3\\nu=199/100.$$ Here $\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(e,y)$ is the minimum supremum distance to the two endpoint sites. The constants are uniform over the graph, $A$, $z$, and the source. Sites with any number of available slots between one and $\\ell$ are permitted.",
          "source_id": "PM-271.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "In an available-slot graph regular at $z$ with radius $A\\ge A_0$, put $$\\mathcal D(v)=\\tfrac12\\sum_{\\{i,j\\}\\text{ available edge}}\n                              (v_i-v_j)^2.$$ Every nonnegative $v$ supported over $B_A(z)$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:local-nash}\n \\lVert v\\rVert_2^{10/3}\\le C\\mathcal D(v)\\lVert v\\rVert_1^{4/3}.$$ For every available slot $a$ at $y\\in B_A(z)$, there exists an oriented edge flow $F_a$, with divergence defined as outgoing minus incoming flow, such that $$\\label{eq:unit-flow}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}F_a=\\mathbf 1_{\\{a\\}},\\qquad\n |F_a(e)|\\le C(1+\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(e,y))^{-k},\\qquad\n k=2-3\\nu=199/100.$$ Here $\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(e,y)$ is the minimum supremum distance to the two endpoint sites.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-torus-blocking",
        "exact_quote": "Under $\\mathsf I(P)$, at any deterministic time $t$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb P_P(A'\\subseteq\\mathcal O_t)&\\le Cp^6\n &&\\text{for six prescribed distinct sites},\\label{eq:six-blocking}\\\\\n \\mathbb P_P(A'\\subseteq\\mathcal O_t)&\\le(C\\sqrt p)^{|A'|}\n &&\\text{for a nonempty sparse set of sites}.\\label{eq:sparse-blocking}\n\\end{aligned}$$ There are $c,C,\\lambda>0$, uniform for sufficiently large $\\beta$, such that the blocked component formed with steps of supremum length at most eight satisfies $$\\label{eq:torus-component-tail}\n \\mathbb P_P\\{z\\in\\mathcal O_t,\\ \n       \\max_{v\\in\\mathcal C_t(z)}d_L(z,v)\\ge h\\}\n       \\le C e^{-ch^\\lambda}\\qquad(h\\ge1).$$ All constants are independent of the grid and its size.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-torus-blocking",
          "exact_quote": "Under $\\mathsf I(P)$, at any deterministic time $t$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb P_P(A'\\subseteq\\mathcal O_t)&\\le Cp^6\n &&\\text{for six prescribed distinct sites},\\label{eq:six-blocking}\\\\\n \\mathbb P_P(A'\\subseteq\\mathcal O_t)&\\le(C\\sqrt p)^{|A'|}\n &&\\text{for a nonempty sparse set of sites}.\\label{eq:sparse-blocking}\n\\end{aligned}$$ There are $c,C,\\lambda>0$, uniform for sufficiently large $\\beta$, such that the blocked component formed with steps of supremum length at most eight satisfies $$\\label{eq:torus-component-tail}\n \\mathbb P_P\\{z\\in\\mathcal O_t,\\ \n       \\max_{v\\in\\mathcal C_t(z)}d_L(z,v)\\ge h\\}\n       \\le C e^{-ch^\\lambda}\\qquad(h\\ge1).$$ All constants are independent of the grid and its size.",
          "source_id": "PM-271.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Under $\\mathsf I(P)$, at any deterministic time $t$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb P_P(A'\\subseteq\\mathcal O_t)&\\le Cp^6\n &&\\text{for six prescribed distinct sites},\\label{eq:six-blocking}\\\\\n \\mathbb P_P(A'\\subseteq\\mathcal O_t)&\\le(C\\sqrt p)^{|A'|}\n &&\\text{for a nonempty sparse set of sites}.\\label{eq:sparse-blocking}\n\\end{aligned}$$ There are $c,C,\\lambda>0$, uniform for sufficiently large $\\beta$, such that the blocked component formed with steps of supremum length at most eight satisfies $$\\label{eq:torus-component-tail}\n \\mathbb P_P\\{z\\in\\mathcal O_t,\\ \n       \\max_{v\\in\\mathcal C_t(z)}d_L(z,v)\\ge h\\}\n       \\le C e^{-ch^\\lambda}\\qquad(h\\ge1).$$ All constants are independent of the grid and its size.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-torus-diffusion",
        "exact_quote": "Assume $\\mathsf I(P)$ and <a href=\"#eq:sparse-localization-radius\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:sparse-localization-radius\">[eq:sparse-localization-radius]</a>. For every deterministic time and anchor, failure of regularity has probability at most $$\\label{eq:torus-irregularity}\n \\Delta_A=CA^3p^6+C\\exp(-cA^{\\lambda\\nu}).$$ Call an exposure good at the anchor if its nonregular times have total length at most $\\beta/100$. Its probability of not being good is at most $100\\Delta_A$. On a good exposure, the one-turn kernel localized by killing outside $B_A(z)$ has every entry at most $C\\beta^{-3/2}$, in either direction.*\n\n*If a deterministic starting slot is a hole almost surely, localization at its site loses at most $Ce^{-c\\beta}$ expected mass in any forward or reversed traversal piece of length at most one turn. This last assertion does not require $\\mathsf I(P)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.3"
      },

Its probability of not being good is at most $100\\Delta_A$. This last assertion does not require $\\mathsf I(P)$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-torus-flow-pairing",
        "exact_quote": "At a fixed slice let $(z_i)_{i=1}^{m_T}$ be a sparse list, including repetitions. Retain any subset of its members at which the lifted graph is regular with radius $A$. For each retained member let $\\mu_i\\ge0$ be a mass at most one, supported on the real holes over $B_A(z_i)$.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-weak-field",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $S$. For all sufficiently large $\\beta$ there are $C_\\beta,R_0(\\beta)<\\infty$ with the following property.       },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-soft-resolvent",
        "exact_quote": "Given the exposure $\\mathbf a$, for $i,j\\in\\mathcal H$, $i\\ne j$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb P(i\\text{ is pinned}\\mid\\mathbf a)\n   &=q(D^{-1})_{ii},\\label{eq:pin-marginal}\\\\\n \\mathbb P(i,j\\text{ are pinned, next pin from }i\\text{ is }j\n            \\mid\\mathbf a)\n   &=\\frac{q^2}{w}(D^{-1})_{ij}.\\label{eq:pin-successor}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $$\\label{eq:resolvent-trace}\n \\mathbb E\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}D^{-1}\\le N.$$ For $f_i=f_{x_i}$ on the slots, $$\\label{eq:transverse-resolvent}\n \\langle D_f^2\\rangle_b\n =\\frac14\\sum_i f_i^2+\n   \\frac12\\mathbb E\\sum_{\\substack{i,j\\in\\mathcal H\\\\i\\ne j}}\n                   f_if_j(D^{-1})_{ij}.$$ All expectations here include the normalized picture and soft-pin law.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.3"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-hull-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "There is a constant $C_\\beta$ such that the following hold for every usable $B$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-strip-curvature",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be holomorphic on $\\{z\\in\\mathbb C:|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Im}}z|<a_0\\}$, real and even on the real axis, with $F(0)=0$. Suppose that, for some $K<\\infty$, $$0\\le F(x)\\le K|x|,\n  \\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{Re}}F(x+iy)\\le F(x)\n  \\quad(x\\in\\mathbb R,\\ |y|<a_0).$$ For each $a\\in(0,a_0)$ there is a strictly positive Schwartz function $J_a$ such that, with $g_a(x)=F(x)-\\mathop{\\mathrm{Re}}F(x+ia)$, $$\\label{eq:strip-convolution}\n F''=J_a*g_a,\n \\qquad\n J_a=\\frac{2}{a^2}(h_a*h_a),\n \\qquad\n h_a(x)=\\frac{\\pi}{2a}\\mathop{\\mathrm{sech}}^2\\!\\left(\\frac{\\pi x}{a}\\right).$$ In particular, with $c_a=2\\pi/a$, $$\\label{eq:strip-curvature-growth}\n  0\\le F''(x)\\le e^{c_a|x|}F''(0),\n  \\qquad\n  F(x)\\le \\frac{F''(0)}{c_a^2}\n       \\bigl(e^{c_a|x|}-1-c_a|x|\\bigr).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-271.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-strip-curvature",
          "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be holomorphic on $\\{z\\in\\mathbb C:|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Im}}z|<a_0\\}$, real and even on the real axis, with $F(0)=0$.           "source_id": "PM-271.3"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-zero-field",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $S$ and a sufficiently large finite $\\beta$. There are $R_0=R_0(S,\\beta)$, $C_\\beta<\\infty$, and $\\kappa>0$ with the following property. Let $L\\ge R\\ge R_0$, with $L$ even, and partition $\\Lambda_L$ into rectangular cells whose side lengths lie in $[R/2,4R]$. If $f:\\Lambda_L\\to[-1,1]$ has sum zero on every cell, then, for $q=R^{-5/2}$, $b=-\\log(1-q)$, and any spin component $a$, $$\\label{eq:zero-exp}\n \\log\\left\\langle\n   \\exp\\left(bx\\sum_{z\\in\\Lambda_L}f_zS_z^a\\right)\n \\right\\rangle\n \\le C_\\beta bV,\n \\qquad x\\in\\mathbb R,\\quad |x|\\le\\kappa\\log R.$$ Consequently there are $A_\\beta,c>0$ such that the spectral measurement of $D_f=\\sum_z f_zS_z^a$ in the zero-field state satisfies $$\\label{eq:zero-tail}\n \\mathbb P\\left\\{|D_f|>\\frac{A_\\beta V}{\\log R}\\right\\}\n       \\le 2e^{-cbV}.$$ All bounds are uniform over the indicated partitions and tests.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-zero-field",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $S$ and a sufficiently large finite $\\beta$. There are $R_0=R_0(S,\\beta)$, $C_\\beta<\\infty$, and $\\kappa>0$ with the following property. Let $L\\ge R\\ge R_0$, with $L$ even, and partition $\\Lambda_L$ into rectangular cells whose side lengths lie in $[R/2,4R]$. If $f:\\Lambda_L\\to[-1,1]$ has sum zero on every cell, then, for $q=R^{-5/2}$, $b=-\\log(1-q)$, and any spin component $a$, $$\\label{eq:zero-exp}\n \\log\\left\\langle\n   \\exp\\left(bx\\sum_{z\\in\\Lambda_L}f_zS_z^a\\right)\n \\right\\rangle\n \\le C_\\beta bV,\n \\qquad x\\in\\mathbb R,\\quad |x|\\le\\kappa\\log R.$$ Consequently there are $A_\\beta,c>0$ such that the spectral measurement of $D_f=\\sum_z f_zS_z^a$ in the zero-field state satisfies $$\\label{eq:zero-tail}\n \\mathbb P\\left\\{|D_f|>\\frac{A_\\beta V}{\\log R}\\right\\}\n       \\le 2e^{-cbV}.$$ All bounds are uniform over the indicated partitions and tests.",
          "source_id": "PM-271.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $S$ and a sufficiently large finite $\\beta$. There are $R_0=R_0(S,\\beta)$, $C_\\beta<\\infty$, and $\\kappa>0$ with the following property. Let $L\\ge R\\ge R_0$, with $L$ even, and partition $\\Lambda_L$ into rectangular cells whose side lengths lie in $[R/2,4R]$. If $f:\\Lambda_L\\to[-1,1]$ has sum zero on every cell, then, for $q=R^{-5/2}$, $b=-\\log(1-q)$, and any spin component $a$, $$\\label{eq:zero-exp}\n \\log\\left\\langle\n   \\exp\\left(bx\\sum_{z\\in\\Lambda_L}f_zS_z^a\\right)\n \\right\\rangle\n \\le C_\\beta bV,\n \\qquad x\\in\\mathbb R,\\quad |x|\\le\\kappa\\log R.$$ Consequently there are $A_\\beta,c>0$ such that the spectral measurement of $D_f=\\sum_z f_zS_z^a$ in the zero-field state satisfies $$\\label{eq:zero-tail}\n \\mathbb P\\left\\{|D_f|>\\frac{A_\\beta V}{\\log R}\\right\\}\n       \\le 2e^{-cbV}.$$ All bounds are uniform over the indicated partitions and tests.",
      "explanation": "At zero field, zero-sum cell tests have an exponential moment bound on a logarithmically long range, giving a tail bound for their spectral measurement.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-scale-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $S$ and a sufficiently large finite $\\beta$. For every sufficiently large fixed $R_*$ and all sufficiently large even $L$, there is a deterministic rectangular partition $\\mathcal P_*(L)$ of $\\Lambda_L$ whose side lengths lie in $[R_*/2,4R_*^{10/9}]$ and such that, for $a=1,2,3$, $$\\label{eq:spatial-comparison}\n \\frac1V\\sum_{C\\in\\mathcal P_*(L)}|C|\n       \\left\\langle(\\overline S_C^a)^2\\right\\rangle\n \\le\n       \\left\\langle(M_L^a/V)^2\\right\\rangle\n       +\\frac{C_\\beta}{\\log R_*}+o_L(1).$$ Here $o_L(1)\\to0$ at fixed $R_*$ and $\\beta$.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-scale-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $S$ and a sufficiently large finite $\\beta$.           "source_id": "PM-271.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $S$ and a sufficiently large finite $\\beta$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-variational-framework",
        "exact_quote": "The mean entropy exists on $\\mathcal S_{\\rm TI}$, is upper semicontinuous and affine, and $$\\label{eq:variational-pressure}\n P_a(t)=\\max_{\\omega\\in\\mathcal S_{\\rm TI}}\n       \\{\\mathfrak s(\\omega)+e_0(\\omega)+t\\omega(a)\\}.$$ In particular, writing $P_0=\\beta p_{S,\\beta}(0)$, the set $$\\mathcal E_\\beta=\n \\{\\omega\\in\\mathcal S_{\\rm TI}:\n            \\mathfrak s(\\omega)+e_0(\\omega)=P_0\\}$$ is a nonempty compact face of $\\mathcal S_{\\rm TI}$. Every local limit of periodic zero-field Gibbs states belongs to $\\mathcal E_\\beta$, and $$\\label{eq:pressure-tangent}\n P_a'(0+)=\\max_{\\omega\\in\\mathcal E_\\beta}\\omega(a).$$ The extreme points of $\\mathcal E_\\beta$ are precisely its states that are extreme in $\\mathcal S_{\\rm TI}$; we call these states ergodic.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "source_id": "PM-271.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The mean entropy exists on $\\mathcal S_{\\rm TI}$, is upper semicontinuous and affine, and $$\\label{eq:variational-pressure}\n P_a(t)=\\max_{\\omega\\in\\mathcal S_{\\rm TI}}\n       \\{\\mathfrak s(\\omega)+e_0(\\omega)+t\\omega(a)\\}.$$ In particular, writing $P_0=\\beta p_{S,\\beta}(0)$, the set $$\\mathcal E_\\beta=\n \\{\\omega\\in\\mathcal S_{\\rm TI}:\n            \\mathfrak s(\\omega)+e_0(\\omega)=P_0\\}$$ is a nonempty compact face of $\\mathcal S_{\\rm TI}$. Every local limit of periodic zero-field Gibbs states belongs to $\\mathcal E_\\beta$, and $$\\label{eq:pressure-tangent}\n P_a'(0+)=\\max_{\\omega\\in\\mathcal E_\\beta}\\omega(a).$$ The extreme points of $\\mathcal E_\\beta$ are precisely its states that are extreme in $\\mathcal S_{\\rm TI}$; we call these states ergodic.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-replica-cross",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $S$ and a sufficiently large finite $\\beta$. Let $\\omega$ be any translation-invariant equilibrium state of $j$ unperturbed replicas. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **pin** ([source](#pm-h-9)): A *pin* is a specified slot-time point required to have up color.
- **stable polynomial** ([source](#eq:stable-permutation-symbol)): A nonzero polynomial is *stable* if it does not vanish when all variables have positive imaginary parts.
- **admissible permutation law** ([source](#pm-h-8)): We call a permutation law on a finite set *admissible* if it is a distributional limit of finite products of independent Bernoulli transpositions and deterministic permutations.
- **blocked site** ([source](#pm-h-21)): A site is *blocked* if it has no cut hole.
- **bad coarse box** ([source](#pm-h-21)): Declare a coarse box *bad* when at least one of the following occurs:
- **usable cell** ([source](#eq:unusable-cell)): Call $B$ *usable* if every component of this finite blocked set has diameter at most $2n$.
- **equilibrium and ergodic state** ([source](#subsec:strategy)): Here an equilibrium state maximizes mean entropy minus $\beta$ times the physical energy density among translation-invariant states; an ergodic state is an extreme point of that invariant state space.

| q-magnetization-lower-bound | spontaneous magnetization | ≥ | $S(1-\beta^{-9/10})$ | Fixed spin, large inverse temperature. |
| q-sparse-pin-rate | per-point pin avoidance factor | = | $\beta^{-9/10}$ | Sparse test sets at one time. |
| q-flow-decay-exponent | unit-flow decay exponent | = | $199/100$ | Radius at least the fixed threshold. |
| q-weak-field-bound | normalized transverse square in small field | ≤ | $C_\beta R^{-1/40}$ | Large inverse temperature, scale at least a threshold. |
| q-cell-resolvent-error | cellwise resolvent approximation error | ≤ | $C_\beta N R^{-1/20}$ | Fixed large inverse temperature, large scale. |
| q-hole-cancellation-bound | expected absolute cell sum over holes | ≤ | $C\sqrt{\|B\|/q}$ | Every cell. |
| q-component-count-bound | expected number of components of the communication graph | ≤ | $qN$ | Soft-pin picture. |
| q-scale-choice-delta | short-flag threshold | = | $R^{-1/8}$ | Large scale R. |
| q-covariance-constant | covariance constant D_B | = | $96c_BB^3+1$ | Block size B and larger cube side at least twice B. |
| q-equilibrium-radius | squared length of equilibrium magnetization vector | = | $m^2$ | Large inverse temperature. |
| q-global-second-moment | normalized global second moment of total spin | ≥ | $m^2$ | Large inverse temperature. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-pin-inputs** [claim](#stmt-lem-pin-inputs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pin-inputs): Stable polynomial deletion of nonpins, negative dependence, exposure disintegration, Poisson insertion. -> claim-finite-pin-inputs
- **step-next-pin** [claim](#stmt-lem-next-pin) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-next-pin): Exterior-power moments and Schur complement. -> claim-conditional-next-pin-kernel
- **step-trace-zero-free** [claim](#stmt-lem-trace-zero-free) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trace-zero-free): Disk contraction of multiaffine polynomials. -> claim-zero-free-layered-traces
- **step-strip-maps** [claim](#stmt-lem-strip-maps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-strip-maps): Upper half-plane picture of single-slot maps. -> claim-single-slot-maps
- **step-torus-blocking** [claim](#stmt-lem-torus-blocking) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-torus-blocking): Factorial domination and recursive binary witnesses. -> claim-blocking-component-tails
- **step-torus-diffusion** [claim](#stmt-lem-torus-diffusion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-torus-diffusion): Nash inequality differential bound and path counting for localization. -> claim-regularity-localized-diffusion
- **step-lattice-inputs** [claim](#stmt-lem-lattice-inputs) / [proof](#stmt-lem-lattice-inputs): Imported deterministic lattice results. -> claim-lattice-nash-unit-flows
- **step-flow-pairing** [claim](#stmt-lem-torus-flow-pairing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-torus-flow-pairing): Tapered unit flows projected to the torus and Cauchy-Schwarz. -> claim-sparse-source-pairing
- **step-sparse-induction** [claim](#stmt-prop-sparse-pins) / [proof](#pm-h-17): Downward induction on pins using return energy. -> claim-sparse-pin-bound
- **step-positive-magnetization** [claim](#stmt-cor-positive-magnetization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-positive-magnetization): Differentiate the tilted exponent pressure and pass to the limit. -> claim-positive-magnetization
- **step-soft-resolvent** [claim](#stmt-lem-soft-resolvent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-soft-resolvent): Determinant multilinearity and Schur complements. -> claim-soft-pin-identities
- **step-hole-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-lem-hole-cancellation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hole-cancellation): Zero-free Laplace transform and Cauchy estimate for the variance. -> claim-hole-cancellation
- **step-skeleton-graph** [claim](#stmt-lem-skeleton-graph) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-skeleton-graph): Lower bounds on kernel entries and a trace argument. -> claim-communication-costs
- **step-bad-box-tail** [claim](#stmt-lem-bad-box-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bad-box-tail): Multiscale witnesses with sparse growth and factorial domination. -> claim-bad-box-tails
- **step-good-box-paths** [claim](#stmt-lem-good-box-paths) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-good-box-paths): Coordinate paths repaired by local connectivity. -> claim-local-paths-good-boxes
- **step-hull-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-hull-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hull-comparison): Imported hull routes, telescoping and Cauchy-Schwarz. -> claim-hull-comparison
- **step-cell-resolvent** [claim](#stmt-lem-cell-resolvent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cell-resolvent): Poincare inequality on cells and error summation. -> claim-cellwise-resolvent
- **step-weak-field** [claim](#stmt-prop-weak-field) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cell-resolvent-2): Combine cellwise approximation with hole cancellation in the transverse identity. -> claim-weak-field-estimate
- **step-strip-curvature** [claim](#stmt-lem-strip-curvature) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-strip-curvature): Fourier analysis of a harmonic function in a strip. -> claim-strip-curvature
- **step-zero-field** [claim](#stmt-prop-zero-field) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-zero-field): Zero-free strip, curvature growth and exponential Markov. -> claim-zero-field-exponential-bound
- **step-scale-comparison** [claim](#stmt-prop-scale-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-scale-comparison): Nested partitions, sign tests, union bound, telescoping. -> claim-scale-comparison
- **step-variational** [claim](#stmt-prop-variational-framework) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-variational-framework): Entropy subadditivity and Gibbs variational principle. -> claim-variational-framework
- **step-ergodic-variance** [claim](#stmt-lem-ergodic-variance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ergodic-variance): Mean ergodic theorem and extremality. -> claim-ergodic-averages
- **step-replica-cross** [claim](#stmt-lem-replica-cross) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-replica-cross): Relative entropy per site, zero-field exponential bound and Golden-Thompson. -> claim-replica-cross-terms
- **step-harmonic-selection** [claim](#stmt-lem-harmonic-selection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-harmonic-selection): Paley-Zygmund, submean inequality, Harnack and diagonal extraction. -> claim-harmonic-selection
- **step-harmonic-tangent** [claim](#stmt-prop-harmonic-tangent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-harmonic-selection-2): Apply the selection lemma to random zero-free traces with replicated moments. -> claim-harmonic-pressure-tangent
- **step-equilibrium-radius** [claim](#stmt-prop-equilibrium-radius) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-equilibrium-radius): Contradiction between analytic continuation and a smaller radius. -> claim-equilibrium-magnetization-radius
- **step-local-second-moment** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-second-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-second-moment): Extreme states plus convexity of the face. -> claim-local-second-moment
- **step-global-second-moment** [claim](#stmt-prop-global-second-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-global-second-moment): Local convergence along a subsequence and scale comparison. -> claim-global-second-moment
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-global-second-moment-2): Total-spin decomposition, upper tail by exponential Markov, concentration and Riemann sums. -> claim-spherical-magnetization-law

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-spherical-law-positive-radius**: Below a spin-dependent threshold temperature, the right zero-field derivative of the pressure is strictly positive. It is the deterministic radius of the limiting spherical magnetization law.
- **inv-positive-magnetization-lower-bound**: For all sufficiently large inverse temperature the spontaneous magnetization is bounded below by a positive explicit expression in the spin and the inverse temperature.
- **inv-sparse-pin-decay**: The probability that a sparse set of test points lies on cycles avoiding all pins decays geometrically in the number of test points, uniformly in the extra pins.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-low-dimension**: Applying the spherical magnetization law to a one- or two-dimensional Heisenberg system at positive temperature. -> The cited no-order theorem excludes spontaneous order there, so no magnetized equilibrium state with positive radius exists.
- **anti-antiferromagnet**: Transferring the spherical law to quantum antiferromagnets. -> The ordering input used in the proof is ferromagnetic and isotropic. The antiferromagnetic results do not supply it.
- **anti-high-temperature**: Quoting a positive deterministic radius at temperatures below the threshold inverse temperature. -> Positivity of the magnetization and the spherical law are asserted only above the spin-dependent inverse-temperature threshold.
- **anti-odd-side-length**: Using the limit along odd side lengths or other boundary conditions. -> The volume limits in the paper run through even side lengths on periodic tori only.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are asymptotic existence statements for a fixed spin and a sufficiently large but unspecified inverse temperature. The proof uses limits of thermodynamic quantities, compactness, extreme-point arguments and constants that are never made explicit. No finite procedure with explicit steps is stated, so no reference implementation can be checked.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper is an existence proof of a limit law with thresholds that are only asserted to exist, so there is no module, interface or runtime behaviour to implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result concerns equilibrium moments of a quantum lattice model, not a time-stepping or real-time dynamical model, and gives no stepping rule that could drive a simulation. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No checker, converter or analysis utility is produced: the lemmas feed a single proof and carry unspecified constants, so nothing can be packaged as a tool. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The permutation, pin and hole-walk arguments are probabilistic analysis devices on a finite picture, not a procedure with inputs, outputs and a cost; constants and thresholds are not computed. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Graphs on holes, coarse boxes and hulls appear only as proof objects whose properties are estimated; no representation, update or query operation is specified. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no kernel, dataflow or arithmetic specification; the trace and contraction lemmas are qualitative nonvanishing statements, not computations to map onto hardware. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, adversary or protocol exists; replica cross terms vanishing is a statement about equilibrium states, not about secrecy or randomness quality. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no data, objective or training procedure; the harmonic selection argument extracts analytic limits from random holomorphic functions and does not define a learning method. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The variational principle is a maximisation over states used to characterise equilibrium, not an optimisation problem to be solved, and no solution method or guarantee on iterates is given. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There are no nodes, messages or fault model; the lattice and random exchanges are physical modelling devices and give no coordination or consensus property. |
| other (Benchmarking of quantum spin simulation) | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The exact limiting transform and second moment give reference values against which a finite-volume spin simulation could be compared, though only after taking the volume limit and only at sufficiently low temperature. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-temperature-threshold** (hypothesis): The spherical law and positivity of the magnetization are asserted only for inverse temperature at or above a spin-dependent threshold. The threshold is shown to exist but is never given explicitly. Blocked use: ['Quoting a positive deterministic radius at inverse temperature below the threshold', 'Using a specific numerical threshold taken from this paper']
- **limit-fixed-spin** (hypothesis): The spin is fixed once and for all and the threshold depends on it; the result is not uniform in the spin. Blocked use: ['Letting the spin grow together with the volume and still applying the law', 'Assuming one threshold serves every spin']
- **limit-even-periodic-cubes** (hypothesis): All volume limits are taken through even side lengths on periodic three-dimensional tori. Blocked use: ['Applying the limit along odd side lengths', 'Transferring the law to open boundary conditions or non-cubic geometries']
- **limit-low-dimension** (hypothesis): The magnetization law is for the three-dimensional lattice. In one and two dimensions earlier work excludes spontaneous order at positive temperature. Blocked use: ['Applying the spherical law to one- or two-dimensional Heisenberg systems at positive temperature']
- **limit-antiferromagnet** (hypothesis): The ordering input is the isotropic ferromagnetic one; quantum antiferromagnetic ordering results do not supply it. Blocked use: ['Transferring the law to antiferromagnetic or frustrated models']
- **limit-order-of-limits** (formal_scope): The pressure is defined by the volume limit first, and only then is the field removed. The lower bound on the magnetization is proved with the volume limit taken before the field goes to zero. Blocked use: ['Interchanging the volume and zero-field limits when comparing with simulations', 'Reading the law at a single small volume']
- **limit-ineffective-constants** (nonconstructive): The constants in the spatial estimates and the thresholds on scale and volume are not made explicit, and the proof uses compactness and extreme-point arguments. No algorithm or explicit bound follows. Blocked use: ['Computing a threshold inverse temperature from the paper', 'Presenting the argument as an executable procedure with a stated cost']
- **limit-sparse-pin-regime** (hypothesis): The sparse-pin bound needs the soft-pin parameter bounded below by a negative power of the side length, a sufficiently large volume and a sparse test set at one time. Blocked use: ['Applying the bound to dense test sets', 'Applying it with a vanishingly small field at fixed volume']
- **limit-zero-field-cells** (hypothesis): The zero-field exponential bound holds for test functions bounded by one with zero sum on every cell of a rectangular partition at scale large enough. Blocked use: ['Applying it to test functions without cellwise cancellation', 'Applying it below the scale threshold']
- **limit-no-joint-measurement** (formal_scope): The statement uses self-adjoint linear combinations of the spin components and makes no joint finite-volume measurement claim for the noncommuting components. Blocked use: ['Interpreting the limit as a joint distribution of the three spin components']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the spherical magnetization law for the three-dimensional nearest-neighbor isotropic quantum Heisenberg ferromagnet at every fixed positive quantum spin and every sufficiently low fixed positive temperature. As the even periodic cubes grow, the symmetric zero-field magnetization converges in moments to a uniform direction with a deterministic positive magnitude. This magnitude equals the right derivative at zero field of the infinite-volume pressure. The law is expressed through self-adjoint linear combinations of the spin components and requires no joint measurement of noncommuting observables.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

An isotropic ferromagnet has no preferred direction in zero field. In a finite periodic volume, its Gibbs state is therefore rotation invariant even at temperatures where magnetized equilibrium states exist. The natural description of the ordered phase is a magnetization of fixed length and uniformly distributed direction. Rotation invariance alone does not establish this description: it allows a mixture of different lengths. The question addressed here is whether the symmetric finite-volume state selects a single length, and whether that length is the magnetization selected by an infinitesimal field.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Model and result

<a id="eq:pressure"></a>
Fix $S\in\{\tfrac12,1,\tfrac32,\ldots\}$. Let $S^1,S^2,S^3$ be the irreducible spin-$S$ matrices, normalized by $$[S^a,S^b]=i\sum_c\epsilon_{abc}S^c,
 \qquad \sum_{a=1}^3(S^a)^2=S(S+1)I.$$ For even $L\ge4$, write $\Lambda_L=(\mathbb Z/L\mathbb Z)^3$ and $V=L^3$. On $\bigotimes_{x\in\Lambda_L}\mathbb C^{2S+1}$, consider <a id="eq:model"></a>$$\label{eq:model}
 H_{S,L}=-\sum_{\{x,y\}\text{ nearest neighbors}}
              \sum_{a=1}^3 S_x^aS_y^a,
 \qquad M_L^a=\sum_{x\in\Lambda_L}S_x^a,$$ where each unordered bond is counted once. We use the Gibbs expectation $$\langle A\rangle_{\beta,L}
 =\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(Ae^{-\beta H_{S,L}})}{\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-\beta H_{S,L}}}.$$ The pressure and its right derivative at zero field are <a id="eq:magnetization"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 p_{S,\beta}(h)
   &=\lim_{\substack{L\to\infty\\L\text{ even}}}
       \frac1{\beta V}\log\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}
          e^{-\beta(H_{S,L}-hM_L^3)},\label{eq:pressure}\\
 m_{S,\beta}&=p_{S,\beta}'(0+).
       \label{eq:magnetization}
\end{aligned}$$ The pressure limit exists for this bounded finite-range interaction, and convexity gives the right derivative. The volume limit in <a href="#eq:pressure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pressure">[eq:pressure]</a> precedes removal of the field.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:spherical-law"></a>
<a id="eq:second-moment-law"></a>
**Theorem 1** (Spherical magnetization law). *For each fixed $S$ there is a finite $\beta_0(S)$ such that, for every fixed finite $\beta\ge\beta_0(S)$, one has $m=m_{S,\beta}>0$ and $$\label{eq:spherical-law}
 \lim_{\substack{L\to\infty\\L\text{ even}}}
  \left\langle
     \exp\!\left(\frac1V\sum_{a=1}^3t_aM_L^a\right)
  \right\rangle_{\beta,L}
   =\frac1{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb S^2}e^{m t\cdot n}\,\mathrm d\sigma(n)
   =\frac{\sinh(m|t|)}{m|t|}
 \qquad(t\in\mathbb R^3).$$ Here $\sigma$ is surface-area measure on $\mathbb S^2$, and the quotient at $t=0$ is interpreted as $1$. In addition, $$\label{eq:second-moment-law}
 \lim_{\substack{L\to\infty\\L\text{ even}}}
   \frac1{V^2}\sum_{x,y\in\Lambda_L}
          \langle S_x\cdot S_y\rangle_{\beta,L}=m^2.$$*

</div>

The statement uses self-adjoint linear combinations of the spin components. It requires no joint finite-volume measurement of these noncommuting operators. Both the spin and the positive temperature remain fixed throughout the volume limit.

<a id="subsec:history"></a>
#### Context and antecedents

The dimensional distinction in this problem is fundamental. Mermin and Wagner (Mermin and Wagner 1966) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[MerminWagner1966](#ref-MerminWagner1966)] excluded spontaneous ferro- or antiferromagnetic order at positive temperature for finite-range isotropic Heisenberg models in dimensions one and two. In higher dimensions, infrared bounds proved continuous-symmetry breaking for classical models (Fröhlich, Simon, and Spencer 1976) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[FSS1976](#ref-FSS1976)] and ordering for quantum antiferromagnets (Dyson, Lieb, and Simon 1978) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[DLS1978](#ref-DLS1978)]. These quantum antiferromagnetic results do not supply the isotropic ferromagnetic ordering input used here. Another line of work establishes the low-temperature free-energy asymptotic predicted by spin-wave theory (Correggi, Giuliani, and Seiringer 2015) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[CGS2015](#ref-CGS2015)]. That asymptotic concerns a different limit from the field derivative or the zero-field magnetization distribution at a fixed positive temperature.

The spherical distribution itself has clear antecedents. Ueltschi’s description of three-dimensional loop models (Ueltschi 2017, sec. 8.1) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Ueltschi2017](#ref-Ueltschi2017)] derives the transform $\sinh(hm)/(hm)$ for the spin-$\tfrac12$ ferromagnet conditional on a conjectured Poisson–Dirichlet law for macroscopic loops. Björnberg, Fröhlich, and Ueltschi (Björnberg, Fröhlich, and Ueltschi 2020, Theorem 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[BFU2020](#ref-BFU2020)] prove the spherical transform for the complete-graph quantum Heisenberg model at arbitrary fixed spin. Our setting has nearest-neighbor interactions on the three-dimensional lattice. We prove its magnetization law without assuming a distribution for macroscopic loop lengths.

The existence of spontaneous magnetization and the identification of its zero-field distribution are distinct questions. The companion manuscript (OpenAI 2026b) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Ord](#ref-Ord)] constructs magnetized equilibrium states for the isotropic nearest-neighbor quantum Heisenberg ferromagnet in dimensions at least three, for every fixed spin at sufficiently low positive temperatures. The companion (OpenAI 2026a) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[BL](#ref-BL)] establishes the low-temperature asymptotic of the pressure-defined magnetization for nonnegative symmetric finite-range interactions whose support generates $\mathbb Z^3$. We use finite exchange and geometric estimates from these works. The present argument adds spatial control on periodic tori and a pressure argument excluding translation-ergodic equilibrium states with different magnetization magnitudes.

The exchange representation builds on permutation and random-walk representations of quantum spins (Powers 1976; Conlon and Solovej 1991) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Powers1976](#ref-Powers1976), [ConlonSolovej1991Representation](#ref-ConlonSolovej1991Representation)], Tóth’s random-stirring representation (Tóth 1993) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Toth1993](#ref-Toth1993)], and its higher-spin formulation through symmetric slots (Nachtergaele 1994) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Nachtergaele1994](#ref-Nachtergaele1994)]. The finite pin estimates used below draw on the stability and negative dependence theory of Borcea, Brändén, and Liggett (Borcea, Brändén, and Liggett 2009) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[BBL2009](#ref-BBL2009)]. Our zero-free matrix products use the contraction principle associated with Asano’s and Ruelle’s developments of Lee–Yang theory (Asano 1970; Ruelle 1971, 1973) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Asano1970](#ref-Asano1970), [Ruelle1971](#ref-Ruelle1971), [Ruelle1973](#ref-Ruelle1973)]; the contraction needed here is proved explicitly. Mean entropy, equilibrium states, and pressure tangents are treated within the variational framework of Lanford and Robinson (Lanford and Robinson 1968a, 1968b) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[LanfordRobinson1968Entropy](#ref-LanfordRobinson1968Entropy), [LanfordRobinson1968Spin](#ref-LanfordRobinson1968Spin)]. We recall the required facts and prove the additional replica and harmonic-selection statements.

<a id="subsec:strategy"></a>
#### The two parts of the proof

<a id="eq:overview-upper"></a>
Here is the obstruction that the proof must overcome. Let $J_L$ denote the total-spin label in the rotation-invariant Gibbs state. Conditional on $J_L$, any component of $M_L$ is uniform on $-J_L,-J_L+1,\ldots,J_L$. A fixed positive field and the definition of $m$ already imply $$\label{eq:overview-upper}
 \mathbb P(J_L/V>m+\epsilon)\longrightarrow0
 \qquad(\epsilon>0).$$ Thus it remains to prove <a id="eq:overview-lower"></a>$$\label{eq:overview-lower}
 \liminf_{L\to\infty}\frac{\mathbb E[J_L(J_L+1)]}{V^2}\ge m^2.$$ Together these bounds force $J_L/V\to m$ in probability and give <a href="#eq:spherical-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:spherical-law">[eq:spherical-law]</a>. Section <a href="#sec:global-spin" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:global-spin">8</a> supplies this last representation-theoretic argument in detail.

Two facts produce the lower bound. First, the squared magnetization averaged on large local boxes is controlled by the squared magnetization averaged on the whole torus, with an error vanishing as the local scale grows. This prevents local magnetization from canceling through spatial variation on larger scales. Second, every translation-ergodic equilibrium state has magnetization vector of length $m$. Here an equilibrium state maximizes mean entropy minus $\beta$ times the physical energy density among translation-invariant states; an ergodic state is an extreme point of that invariant state space. Their variational and averaging properties are recalled in Section <a href="#sec:equilibrium" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:equilibrium">6</a>. The second fact identifies the local magnetization length to which the first fact applies.

<a id="eq:overview-exp"></a>
Both facts use the same weak exponential estimate. To describe it, let $\mathcal P$ be a rectangular partition whose cell sides are comparable to a large scale $R\le L$. For a deterministic site function $f$ with $|f_x|\le1$ and $\sum_{x\in C}f_x=0$ in each cell, put $D_f=\sum_xf_xS_x^3$. We prove, for $b_R\asymp R^{-5/2}$ and a fixed $\kappa>0$, that $$\label{eq:overview-exp}
 \log\langle e^{\theta_R D_f}\rangle_{\beta,L}
       \le C_\beta b_R V,
 \qquad \theta_R=\kappa b_R\log R.$$ In particular the left side divided by $\theta_R V$ tends to zero uniformly in these tests. In nested rectangular partitions, apply this bound to signs of differences between a cell average and its parent average. A union bound and a telescoping identity then control all spatial scales. Applied to a product of two local zero-sum spin tests in different replicas, it forces the expectation of that product to vanish in every equilibrium state of the replicated system: finite-volume restrictions of such a state have relative entropy per site tending to zero with respect to the product Gibbs state.

Obtaining <a href="#eq:overview-exp" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:overview-exp">[eq:overview-exp]</a> is the spatial part of the proof. Sections <a href="#sec:exchange" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:exchange">2</a>–<a href="#sec:spatial-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:spatial-comparison">[sec:spatial-comparison]</a> use exchange cycles and a field of strength $b_R$ in a perpendicular direction to bound $R^{-5/2}V^{-1}\langle D_f^2\rangle_{b_R}$ by a negative power of $R$. The field gives a killed one-particle kernel an invertible resolvent. A sparse-pin estimate controls the holes left after exposing unpinned cycles; a comparison on this random set then uses the zero sum of $f$ in each cell. Section <a href="#sec:zero-field" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:zero-field">5</a> turns the resulting variance bound into <a href="#eq:overview-exp" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:overview-exp">[eq:overview-exp]</a> through a zero-free complex strip.

The remaining argument identifies the magnitude. Negative squares of large-box magnetization averages would distinguish states of different lengths, but the corresponding perturbed Gibbs operators do not directly fit the exchange zero-freeness argument. Section <a href="#sec:pressure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pressure">7</a> realizes these perturbations as first-order averages of random products of real matrices acting on the $2S$ spin-$\tfrac12$ slots representing each site. Let $Z(z)$ be the normalized trace of such a product with complex field $z$. For every realization, $Z(z)$ is nonzero on $\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}z>0$. Shared Gaussian variables couple its moments to replicated pressures; the zero-sum cross terms just described disappear from their pressure derivatives. Moment bounds then select harmonic limits of normalized $\log|Z(z)|$. Analyticity of their restrictions to the real axis excludes coexistence of different ergodic magnetization lengths. More concretely, an ergodic state of length $a$, oriented along the auxiliary field, contributes the affine function $xa-a^2$ to a limiting pressure tangent. Large positive $x$ selects the branch $xm-m^2$; analytic continuation of that branch cannot dominate a competing branch of smaller length at every positive $x$. The spatial estimate, the replica argument, and the passage from random zero-free functions to harmonic pressure tangents are the main additional constructions of this paper.

<a id="subsec:conventions"></a>
#### Conventions

We suppress $S$ in expectations and usually suppress $\beta,L$ when no confusion is possible. Write $X,Y,Z$ for the site spin components $1,2,3$, in the standard representation where $X,Z$ are real and $Y$ is imaginary. Set $H_0=-\beta H_{S,L}$. Constants $c,C$ may depend on the fixed spin and on fixed geometric choices; $C_\beta$ may also depend on the fixed inverse temperature. We first choose a sufficiently large finite $\beta$, and only then choose lower thresholds for $L$ and spatial scales. All torus volume limits are through even $L$.

The finite exchange inputs are developed in Section <a href="#sec:exchange" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:exchange">2</a>, and the deterministic lattice estimates imported from the companions are stated with their hypotheses where they are used. Positivity of $m$ follows directly from our torus sparse-pin estimate in Corollary <a href="#cor:positive-magnetization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:positive-magnetization">11</a>; no sharp low-temperature asymptotic is needed. We increase $\beta_0(S)$ as needed below.

<a id="sec:exchange"></a>
### Exchange cycles, pins, and zero-free traces

The exchange representation gives two finite-dimensional tools for the proof. Pinning and exposing cycles turns a return probability into the first moment of a Markov hitting kernel. Stability of the same exchange operators also gives zero-free traces after insertion of suitable single-slot matrices. We develop both tools here, keeping track of the conditioning in the first and the trace contraction in the second.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Symmetric slots and permutation layers

Put $\ell=2S$ and let $\mathcal I=\Lambda_L\times\{1,\ldots,\ell\}$ be the set of $N=\ell V$ slots. On $(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes\mathcal I}$ write $s_i^a$ for the Pauli matrices divided by two, and let $$P_* =\prod_{x\in\Lambda_L}\frac1{\ell!}
                  \sum_{\pi\in\mathfrak S_\ell}\mathcal U_{\pi,x}$$ be the projection onto the tensor product of the symmetric site subspaces. Here $\mathcal U_{\pi,x}$ permutes the slots at $x$. On this subspace $S_x^a=\sum_{i:\,x_i=x}s_i^a$ is the spin-$S$ representation. In particular, if compression is needed for an individual slot operator, then <a id="eq:compressed-slot"></a>$$\label{eq:compressed-slot}
        P_*s_i^aP_* = \ell^{-1}S_{x_i}^aP_*.$$

<a id="eq:exchange-operator"></a>
For each site bond use all $\ell^2$ slot edges above it. On these edges put independent Poisson exchanges of rate $1/2$ during $[0,\beta)$, and at the seam from $\beta$ to $0$ insert an independent uniform slot permutation at every site. Let $\pi$ be the resulting permutation of $\mathcal I$ and $\mathbb P_{\rm b}$ its underlying picture law. If $\mathcal E$ is the set of slot edges, then $$\label{eq:exchange-operator}
 e^{H_0}P_*=e^{\beta|\mathcal E|/4}
                    \mathbb E_{\rm b}\mathcal U_\pi,
 \qquad H_0=-\beta H_{S,L}.$$ On the left $H_0$ is lifted to the slot space before compression. Indeed, $\boldsymbol s_i\cdot\boldsymbol s_j=\frac12\mathcal U_{(ij)}-\frac14I$, and the mean chronological Poisson product has generator $\frac12\sum_{e\in\mathcal E}(\mathcal U_e-I)$. The all-slot sum above each bond commutes with $P_*$, while the seam average inserts $P_*$. This proves <a href="#eq:exchange-operator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exchange-operator">[eq:exchange-operator]</a>. This is Tóth’s exchange representation (Tóth 1993) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Toth1993](#ref-Toth1993)], with the higher-spin construction of Nachtergaele (Nachtergaele 1994, sec. 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Nachtergaele1994](#ref-Nachtergaele1994)]; see also (OpenAI 2026a, Proposition 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[BL](#ref-BL)].

If $A$ is a polynomial in the physical site spins, let $\widehat A$ be its slot lift obtained by replacing $S_x^a$ by $\sum_{i:\,x_i=x}s_i^a$. The physical trace is then <a id="eq:physical-slot-trace"></a>$$\label{eq:physical-slot-trace}
       \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\rm phys}(Ae^{H_0})
          =\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\rm slot}(\widehat A e^{H_0}P_*).$$ Following lines through repetitions of one sampled picture gives its cycles. In this slot trace with diagonal spin insertions a cycle has a constant up or down color. Without insertions its contribution is two. We take slot-time points immediately after an instantaneous operation, including the seam. In finite volume there are almost surely finitely many marks, with distinct times avoiding any prescribed finite set of cuts.

We call a permutation law on a finite set *admissible* if it is a distributional limit of finite products of independent Bernoulli transpositions and deterministic permutations. Products here are ordered; no commutation is assumed.
